#1437. Who should replace Michael Carrick as Man United boss as pressure mounts? Bringing in Unai Emery would be the best thing INEOS have done, writes NATHAN SALT... but would Eddie Howe simply be Carrick 2.0?

Published: 18:06 GMT, 26 October 2026 | Updated: 20:10 GMT, 26 October 2026

Michael Carrick is on the brink of the sack and the sorry cycle of managerial churn in the corridors of Old Trafford is set to pick up once again.

Fans turned on key figures such as director of football Jason Wilcox and CEO Omar Berrada in the closing stages of Sunday’s 3-0 defeat to AFC Bournemouth and they will be under enormous amounts of pressure to get the next appointment right.

Behind the scenes there has long been a difference of opinion on which direction to go next.

As exclusively reported by Daily Mail Sport, Wilcox and director of recruitment Christopher Vivell have been at odds as tensions grow between the pair, with Vivell pushing for former Bayern Munich and Germany manager Julian Nagelsmann, while Wilcox wants a Premier League proven manager. Eddie Howe is his preferred candidate.

So, with that in mind, Daily Mail Sport assesses the five candidates on the club’s internal shortlist to see who the best fit for Manchester United, now at such a low ebb just above the relegation zone, is…

Fans turned on key figures such as director of football Jason Wilcox and CEO Omar Berrada in the closing stages of Sunday’s 3-0 defeat to AFC Bournemouth

The front-runner as it stands given he is the preferred choice of the director of football.

There is an irony in that given former director of football, Dan Ashworth, lobbied for Howe before Ruben Amorim arrived, only to find himself out of a job for not agreeing to hire the Portuguese boss.

That aside, Howe’s appeal is that there is no compensation required to get him as he currently continues his break from the game after leaving Newcastle following a successful five years in the North East in which he ended their 70-year trophy drought with a League Cup.

Privately, Howe is keeping his cards close to his chest but he knows that he checks a lot of the boxes, not least that he’s shown he can cut it in the Premier League and Champions League.

One of Howe’s biggest strengths is that he’s viewed as someone who can build a team from the ground up and privately, figures at United believe he has a track record of improving players through coaching on the training ground.

United sought a low-key character after the fireworks of Amorim but numerous figures close to the dressing room are keen to see an inspirational leader walk through the door and that isn’t really Howe’s MO.

The next manager needs to galvanise a club that is hurtling towards the bottom three with an apathy kicking in that this doomed group are merely rinsing and repeating the same problems.

Where other contenders such as Antonio Conte would achieve that in the short term, Howe’s temperament is much closer to Carrick’s and that may be one reason why some behind the scenes will be hesitating.

Wilcox is a fan and he has enormous sway – for now at least – on the appointment of the next manager. Howe is available to start straight away and won’t cost a penny in compensation. For a club as cheap as United, that’s worth its weight in gold.

Howe’s temperament is much closer to Carrick’s and that may be one reason why some behind the scenes are hesitating

The other leading contender, pushed heavily by Vivell in the boardroom.

Nagelsmann’s stock took a hit after a disastrous World Cup in charge of Germany but even with that, there has long been a fascination in England about the suave 39-year-old.

He has long been viewed as one of Europe’s rising young stars on the coaching scene and therefore it’s no surprise to know that Chelsea, Liverpool and Tottenham Hotspur have all looked at him when job openings came up.

Tactically he is incredibly detailed and is closer to the Amorim and Ten Hag mould, where Howe, from a personality point, is closer to that of Carrick.

Nagelsmann is also free to begin work right away – a key reason he too is near the front of the queue – and one feather in his cap is that he’s had two enormous high-pressure jobs previously in Bayern Munich and Germany.

He could walk into a dressing room and not be overawed by what he finds having had the two biggest jobs in German football and that shouldn’t be underestimated given the next man is walking into a dressing room completely devoid of confidence and belief.

It was Monday when club captain Bruno Fernandes was liking a post on social media claiming he had been pushed to a point where he no longer cares.

‘I strongly believe that if you want to be successful, empathy and taking care of the person behind the player is of greater importance than any tactical aspects,’ Nagelsmann said in his early managerial days.

‘I place great emphasis in giving my players a clear tactical plan to help and support in match situations. But the relationship I have with them is very, very important to me.’

He’s never managed in the Premier League and the worry is that this could be Amorim Mark II, even with so much potential behind him.

This one comes down to the power battle between Wilcox and Vivell and it’s unlikely one the latter will win.

A Premier League job feels inevitable and he’s got lots of admirers in the building at United. This just feels like this could be the wrong timing given many at United are still reeling from the gamble they took on an up-and-coming manager in Amorim.

Tactically Nagelsmann is incredibly detailed and is closer to the Amorim and Ten Hag mould, where Howe, from a personality point, is closer to that of Carrick

The last of the out of work managers to have seen their name crop up in discussions about who and what comes next.

One thing to say is that Conte wants the job and nobody on the shortlist would inject as much short-term adrenaline into the entire club than the Italian. He’d put rockets up all the right people to get a much-needed response.

The issue is that he’s highly combustible and while Sir Jim Ratcliffe has previously gravitated to those who challenge him – Amorim did that and Ratcliffe liked him for it – this feels like a candidate that may be a bridge too far for the thinner-skinned execs put in place by Ratcliffe.

‘My name has been linked with numerous clubs, but there are currently no negotiations at an advanced stage,’ Conte wrote on Instagram in the past week following a damning report in Italian paper La Repubblica.

‘I have read reports of financial demands and requests for full control that I have supposedly already made to the clubs mentioned, as well as reservations about what has been described as my "strong personality". These reports are unfounded.’

Conte is a hybrid candidate that can point to tangible success in England at both Chelsea, where he won the Premier League, and at Tottenham where, for as toxic as it ended, he wasn’t wrong about the issues that the club badly needed to fix.

Players in the dressing room are looking for an inspirational leader to get behind – that’s a quality numerous sources have put to Daily Mail Sport in recent hours and days – and Conte is undoubtedly the man for that job.

He’s won the Premier League, won Serie A, won the FA Cup, and has a resume that he knows what he’s doing.

But the current United structure is very rigid, and the manager isn’t the ultimate shot-caller.

Also, this squad would need major surgery to get it close to playing the intense football Conte would want to play. Feels very unlikely.

Even with his statement dismissing a lot of the concerns attached to his name, he’s seen as too combustible to fit in around all the cooks in the kitchen that is this shambolic boardroom.

Players in the dressing room are looking for an inspirational leader to get behind and Conte is undoubtedly the man for that job

INEOS continue to preach a desire to bring a ‘best in class’ mantra to Manchester United and as laughable as that is given their work to date, bringing in Unai Emery would be the best thing they’ve done to this point.

Emery is at the very top when it comes to the Premier League’s managerial rankings given what he’s done at Aston Villa, who are a major player in the Champions League so far this season.

Villa have no desire to lose Emery – naturally – and while his release clause, if one exists, is no doubt so sky high that it’s prohibitive from United coming in and poaching the Villans' beacon of light in the dugout.

Emery works in a very particular way and that is also a question mark, beyond the enormous compensation that would be required. It would likely usurp the £20million Chelsea had to pay Brighton to get Graham Potter and his staff.

The Spaniard has an enormous amount of power and that would have to be curbed at United unless they went in a totally different direction: one that bestows all of the power to the manager.

His colder personality, compared to say, Howe, is a mark against his name when it comes to getting buy-in from a dressing room that grumble at the most minor inconvenience.

He will try his luck at one of the game’s top, top jobs at some stage but United feels like a stretch given, at the very least, they won’t put the money up for compensation, never mind back him sufficiently in the transfer window to overhaul a squad that is failing miserably.

A top, top manager and perhaps the best candidate on the shortlist given everything he’s achieved. He wrongly gets mocked for his command of English but he’s tactically immense and can make a credible case to be the No1 boss in the Premier League. He’s just too expensive, and United’s hierarchy is notoriously cheap.

Emery has an enormous amount of power and that would have to be curbed at United unless they went in a totally different direction, one that bestows all of the power to the manager

The candidate of choice for Wilcox if money was no object.

Hurzeler is the hot young coach taking the Premier League by storm now, with Brighton in the top three with five wins from the first eight games.

At just 33, he’s a rising star and he’s shown no signs of being overwhelmed since arriving as the Premier League’s youngest-ever coach when he pitched up at 31 from St Pauli in 2024.

Hurzeler spoke earlier this season after knocking Carrick’s United out of the Carabao Cup about how powerful the Brighton structure is and that cannot be underestimated. Everyone is so aligned it has allowed him to focus on coaching, which he’s doing to great success.

The German has built an aggressive, intense, high-press side at Brighton that so often runs over their opponents, all qualities that continually seem to be lacking from United’s sides in recent seasons.

Hurzeler is innovative, exciting and looks to have a bit of steel to him as well. There was a point last season where fans jeered and booed him, and privately some players had their doubts, but he never did as he turned it around.

The thorough dismantling of current league champions Arsenal earlier this season was a sign of how far he’s come and that he can mix it with the best, even with much more limited resources.

But compensation for Hurzeler would likely surpass the £20million it took to get Potter out given Hurzeler signed a three-year contract extension in May.

There are those close to him, too, that have intimated that he is the right coach at the wrong time and that he is not interested in jumping off the Brighton ship right now, not least given he has the chance to deliver European success in the Conference League, as well as a top five finish this campaign.

United’s squad is miles away from what Hurzeler needs to make his vision a success at Old Trafford but his stock is unlikely to diminish anytime soon and so don’t be surprised if Hurzeler eventually finds his way to Old Trafford… it just won’t be now.

Brighton are clicking on every front right now and are a club heading in the right direction, where Manchester United are not. Opportunities for top jobs will keep coming and so he won’t have the overwhelming sense of ‘now or never’ like others do. He’s also just way too expensive right now.

#1434. Gattuso: ‘Make Lazio fans proud, but with 11 players like me, you won’t win much’

Gennaro Gattuso is ‘proud’ so many Lazio fans made the trip for tonight’s Sassuolo match, hails Alberto Aquilani, but warns ‘if you have 11 Gattuso players, you won’t win much!’

It kicks off at the Mapei Stadium in Reggio Emilia at 17.30 GMT (18.30 CET).

You can follow all the build-up and action as it happens from this game, Roma-Cagliari and Torino-Como on the Serie A liveblog.

This is the first midweek round of the season, and therefore particularly unusual for a Lazio side who are not in Europe and crashed out of the Coppa Italia in the opening round.

Lazio fans are boycotting home games and have done for over a year now, but they flock to the away fixtures, so are here in numbers this evening, reported to be anywhere from 5,000 to 10,000.

“It makes us proud,” Gattuso told DAZN Italia.

“Naturally, we need to be ready, as Sassuolo are a team who play well, systematically have four attacking players and go on the attack very quickly.

“It’s a Tuesday early evening, we are grateful that they have come all this way to be by our side and hope to make them happy.”

It really will feel like a home game, because Sassuolo ultras announced that in protest against the kick-off time and cost of this midweek round, they will wait outside the stadium for the first 15 minutes of the match, urging others to do the same.

Gattuso was teammates with Alberto Aquilani at Milan in the 2011/12 campaign, his final season before hanging up his boots.

“I have a great rapport with him. Aquilani was always a very intelligent player and is carrying that on in his coaching career. He takes a lot of risks, is very positive, I feel like Sassuolo deserved more points than they currently have, and I am not surprised. I am sure he’ll have a great career.”

Lazio midfielder Reda Belahyane said this week that they all want to copy the example of Italy World Cup winner Gattuso when playing, but the coach joked about his lack of skill.

“If you have 11 Gattuso players in your line-up, you won’t win much! I think we are working hard, we show unity and play as a team, both when attacking and defending. If we keep that up, we can go far, but if we try to be fancy, don’t help each other out or lose humility, we will struggle. That is the secret.”

#1433. The contribution of salt marshes to wave attenuation during extreme storms

Nature, Published online: 21 October 2026; doi:10.1038/s41586-026-11145-8

Salt marsh platforms resist erosion under extreme waves, but vegetation damage reduces wave attenuation; substantial wave attenuation by vegetation is mainly limited to low-energy marsh coasts with waves below 1 m.

#1432. Röhl Discusses Right-Back Target & Everton Cup Hopes

Merlin Röhl has reflected on a “frustrating” defeat away to Arsenal – but says he’s enjoying his minutes on the field and relishing the chance to learn from the right-back position.

Röhl started at full-back at the Emirates Stadium on Saturday – a 2-0 loss to Mikel Arteta’s reigning Premier League champions.

“Always when you lose a game, there’s frustration,” he told evertontv. “You want to give everything on the pitch and always choose the right choice.

“I think we tried to find our way back into the game. We tried to defend everything, every corner.

“The one corner we didn’t get decides the game.”

Looking ahead, Everton face a Carabao Cup fourth-round clash against Newcastle United on Thursday (7.45pm GMT).

Röhl says the Blues are especially keen for success in the cups this season, explaining it’s something his side “have their eye on”.

“It’s a big game for us,” he continued. “I think before the season, in our squad, we made it clear the Carabao Cup is something we want to go 100 per cent on.

“Of course, we always want to go 100 per cent, but it’s something we have our eye on.

The fans’ hunger for success is another driving factor behind Thursday’s fixture, says Röhl, who added: “It motivates everybody in our team and myself also. It pushes me to – now get the position as right-back – and give everything for the team and improve in that position.

“[I want] to give something the fans love, running up and down and try to win every duel.

“I know it’s not always possible, but they’re the expectations and I love it.”

With Chelsea and Manchester City facing the same obstacle, the Premier League clubs could be forced to look elsewhere unless the situation changes considerably in the coming months.

#1429. Ballon d'Or plunged into CHAOS as esteemed event announces Harry Kane, Kylian Mbappe and Lamine Yamal ALL as winners on official website just hours before the ceremony

Published: 17:29 GMT, 26 October 2026 | Updated: 20:20 GMT, 26 October 2026

Harry Kane, Kylian Mbappe and Lamine Yamal were all bizarrely listed as winners of the Ballon d'Or on the organisers' website hours before the ceremony began in London.

The trio have all been viewed among the leading candidates to land football's biggest individual prize for the first time.

Prior to the event at the London Palladium, the Ballon d'Or's official website appeared to mistakenly publish three separate articles announcing them individually as the winner.

Daily Mail Sport alerted the organisers to the mistake at 5.23pm and it triggered a series of frantic phone calls and WhatsApp messages.

Shocked organisers have since launched an investigation, with articles announcing Kane and Mbappe as winners subsequently being removed.

The article listing Yamal as the winner was still live on the website over 30 minutes after organisers were informed by Daily Mail Sport.

Ballon d'Or organisers published three articles announcing different winners of the Ballon d'Or, including England captain Harry Kane

Lamine Yamal and Kylian Mbappe were also listed as winners on the website, with shocked organisers only becoming aware after being alerted by Daily Mail Sport

A Ballon d’Or statement read: 'Portraits of some Ballon d’Or nominees prepared by were inadvertently published online.

'These articles had been prepared in advance for all nominees in the event of a win.

'They DO NOT, in any way, reveal the podium, which remains unknown to everyone at this time.'

Kane's article featured the headline 'Harry Kane wins 2026 Ballon d’Or: Here are his best and most meaningful strikes from a 61-goal season.'

The article examined Kane's stunning year as he helped Bayern Munich win a domestic Treble, as well as reaching a century of goals for the German side in record time.

'A record-breaking welter of league and cup goals in 2025/26 has propelled Harry Kane to Ballon d’Or success. Here we identify his most iconic and greatest finishes in what has been a bountiful year,' the article stated.

Another announced Yamal as the Ballon d'Or winner, stating that the Barcelona and Spain star had set an 'unprecedented record' by becoming its youngest winner and labelling him as the 'new king of football'.

'In fact, at just 19 years of age, Lamine Yamal has just become the youngest winner in the history of the Ballon d'Or, which was established in 1956,' the article read.

'To truly appreciate the scale of his achievement, one need only recall that not even Lionel Messi – the record holder for the most trophies – had managed to win the Ballon d'Or at that age.

'The Argentine star won it for the first time in 2009, when he was 22 years and 160 days old.

'The all-time record for the youngest winner, until this magical evening in London, belonged to Ronaldo Nazário, who won the Ballon d'Or at the age of 21 years and 92 days.

All three articles were subsequently taken down, with Yamal's last to be removed from the website over 30 minutes after organisers were informed of the error

'Now, this historic record belongs to the new king of football and the brand-new winner of the most prestigious trophy on the planet.

'It belongs to Lamine Yamal Nasraoui Ebana.'

The third stated Mbappe as the winner, lauding him as 'consistency personified at the very highest level'.

'A record-breaking World Cup and another welter of goals for Los Blancos has brought Kylian Mbappe his first Ballon d'Or triumph,' the article read.

'Here we celebrate the French star's consistency, not just last season, but every season since first emerging as a true phenomenon.'

The error took place ahead of official coverage of the ceremony, which will be headlined by the winner of the Ballon d'Or.

England captain Kane is bidding to become the first English player to win the award since Michael Owen in 2001.

Yamal and Mbappe are also hoping to claim the gong for the first time, with the pair having had best finishes of second and third respectively.

#1426. Multimodal precision diagnostics in clinical xenotransplantation

Thank you for visiting nature.com. You are using a browser version with limited support for CSS. To obtain the best experience, we recommend you use a more up to date browser (or turn off compatibility mode in Internet Explorer). In the meantime, to ensure continued support, we are displaying the site without styles and JavaScript.

Nature volume 658, pages 883–896 (2026) Cite this article

Recent breakthroughs in pig-to-human xenotransplantation have successfully prevented hyperacute rejection of kidney, heart, liver and lung xenografts, marking a milestone long considered a critical barrier. However, these major advances also brought new challenges into focus, as immune-mediated graft injuries including antibody-mediated rejection, T cell-mediated rejection and innate immune activation have been observed in most xenotransplantation procedures to date. Detection and management of these injuries remain a critical bottleneck, as conventional histopathology and standard clinical monitoring lack the sensitivity required to identify subclinical injury early enough to guide timely intervention. This diagnostic gap underscores an urgent need for the development of advanced, multimodal precision diagnostic frameworks to enhance immune surveillance, which will be essential for standardizing clinical protocols and enabling pathogenesis-driven therapeutic intervention. This approach will inform both immunosuppressive regimens and future gene editing strategies aimed at engineering rejection-resistant porcine organs, ultimately prolonging graft survival. Broad adoption of such an integrative strategy will be essential to transition xenotransplantation from an experimental intervention into a safe, scalable and patient-centred clinical therapy. In this Review, we synthesize recent progress in xenotransplantation and position multimodal precision diagnostics as a pivotal strategy to address the remaining immunological and clinical barriers to widespread clinical implementation.

#1425. Spalletti reacts to ‘frightening’ Kane’s 2026 Ballon d’Or award: ‘How it should be’

Juventus head coach Luciano Spalletti has had his say on Harry Kane’s triumph at the 2026 Ballon d’Or ceremony, labelling his numbers ‘frightening’ and claiming players who don’t like to make a big fuss of themselves should be the ones recognised: ‘That’s how it should be’.

England international Kane was crowned the winner of the Ballon d’Or award at the 2026 ceremony in London on Monday evening, fending off competition from the likes of Kylian Mbappe, Lamine Yamal, Rodri and Lionel Messi.

Reactions and messages of congratulations have been flooding in since the award was presented on Monday evening, and even Juventus head coach Spalletti was asked for his thoughts on Kane’s victory during his pre-match press conference on Tuesday.

Spalletti was asked what he made of Kane’s victory and whether he feels any of his current players could compete for the biggest individual prize in world football in future years.

He replied: “My players and I have other goals to talk about.”

He did have a few complimentary things to say about Kane, though: “I talked about it with my staff and we thought it was right that it was given to him. It was a logical choice given the quality of the player. He has frightening numbers, even if he’s not always highlighted like some other players. That’s how it should be.”

Click here to read Spalletti’s pre-Genoa press conference in full.

Peter Young is a Senior Reporter with Football Italia.

#1422. Five Premier League clubs in attendance to watch 12 goal striker bag another triple

Club Brugge rising star Nicolo Tresoldi is the next hot property in the Premier League, and we expect major bids next summer.

If you enjoy CaughtOffside coverage and want to see more of it, add us as a preferred source on Google to make us a favourite and see more of our content.

There is never a time when top teams are not looking for a new striker. Regular scorers are the hardest thing to find in the modern game, and when someone even with the slightest potential to be a goal machine pops up in a smaller league, it doesn’t take long for a Premier League side to take a punt.

Just look at Viktor Gyokeres, who scored so many in Portugal that Arsenal eventually had to try their luck.

There are a few names we expect to come to England in the next year or so, but there’s perhaps nobody with a profile rising faster than that of Nicolo Tresoldi. Club Brugge are on an incredible run of developing talent and selling it on, and it looks like they’ve got another gem on their hands.

Tresoldi bagged another three goals in the Belgian league at the weekend, and according to TeamTalk there were no fewer than five Premier League clubs with scouts in attendance.

They say that Chelsea and Man City are the most interested right now, with Arsenal, Tottenham and Newcastle also watching.

Brugge want a club record fee for the German international. They got €40m for Christos Tzolis in the summer, and it’s not crazy to suggest they could ask for twice that if Tresoldi continued scoring at his current rate. He has an insane 12 goals in 13 appearances this season.

#1421. Champions League Vibe Check: Barcelona, Bayern Munich impress; Manchester United in trouble

If not listed, please contact your TV provider.

The UEFA Champions League will transition into midseason mode this week with several key matchups that will test teams' abilities to make deep runs in this season's competition – or win the whole thing by late spring.

Two games in, a few familiar themes are emerging. Back-to-back champions Paris Saint-Germain remain the team to beat while Real Madrid continue to find a way out of trouble, even if wins should feel more elusive to the Spanish giants. Not everyone is in trademark form, though, fascinating subplots emerging as they each begin to chart a way to the podium. Perennial contenders Bayern Munich, for example, hit a stumbling block on Matchday 2 with a 3-3 draw at Viking while Arsenal did not cruise to victory over the weekend.

That does not exactly spell bad news for Bayern or the Gunners, the pair amongst a few teams to keep an eye on as the pursuit of Europe's top club prize continues. While they face each other in an all-important clash in Munich on Wednesday, a pair of formidable foes for the title will meet on Tuesday – PSG host a Barcelona side that may have turned over a new leaf. That said, there is bad news for some after a challenging European week, especially as another series of tests awaits.

Here's a vibe check heading into Matchday 3 of the Champions League.

Every match streams live on Paramount+, your home for every Champions League game all season long, plus replays, highlights, and studio coverage breaking down the race for the top eight. If you haven't signed up yet, now's the time.

Between their financial woes and a propensity to concede goals in recent years, Barcelona have been the neutral's problematic favorite. Hansi Flick's side are still a must-watch this season but for all the right reasons, swapping a chaos factor for classic entertainment headlined by Lamine Yamal. The 19-year-old is box office week in and week out, following up his pair of assists at Galatasaray on Matchday 2 with a goal and an assist in a 5-2 win at Real Betis on Saturday. The goal is worth watching on a loop, his strike from the edge of the penalty area a reminder that his ability to hit a ball is just as impressive as his creative tendencies. Barcelona are beginning to couple their attacking brilliance, though, with more defensive resolve than they have demonstrated since Flick took over two years ago. They have been serial contenders for the Champions League but more than a decade after they last won it, they might actually be in the best position yet to return to the summit. Tuesday's clash at Paris Saint-Germain will really put that theory to the test.

THAT IS ABSOLUTELY OUTRAGEOUS FROM LAMINE YAMAL 😱 pic.twitter.com/vJb1FyQoEa

Vincent Kompany's Bayern Munich resemble Flick's Barcelona in a few ways, including an ability to be lax at the back. They can usually survive blunders at the back because of their sheer attacking might, Ballon d'Or nominees Harry Kane and Michael Olise amongst the most talented and watchable players in the game. It does not mean those defensive errors have not proven costly – Manuel Neuer's risky style ensured they went down early at Augsburg a week ago and only rescued a point out of that game, while their 3-3 draw at Viking on Matchday 2 only added to their overall woes. Bayern, though, have a habit of overcoming those challenges more often than not and responded to two consecutive draws with a 6-1 win over RB Leipzig, a nice confidence booster before Wednesday's clash with Arsenal.

Sure, Sunday's 1-0 win at Nottingham Forest was unglamorous and they currently sit three points behind Premier League leaders Manchester City but Arsenal are more or less exactly where they are expected to be at this stage of the season. The likes of Kai Havertz and Bukayo Saka may have been stumped by Forest's defense, which is a bit sturdier than the results suggest, but it ultimately was not that much of an issue because of the team's depth. Noni Madueke was able to come off the bench and get the job done, only adding to the intrigue ahead of Wednesday's trip to Munich.

Noni Madueke gives Arsenal the lead in the 80th minute with a deflected strike after a beautiful stepover! pic.twitter.com/9RZzf5x1Rl

Manchester United cannot seem to solve their problems, mediocrity their defining quality even if it should not be. The underlying numbers in the Premier League suggest they should be better than 13th place but their attack sputters constantly and they are just leaky enough at the back to dig themselves into holes frequently. Another imperfect day always feels right around the corner for the Red Devils, no matter the occasion. Wednesday's trip to Como will be worth keeping an eye on for that reason, even if the opponents feel a step below Serie A's elites. The higher-ups may have forced an apology out of him but maybe Lisandro Martinez had a point…

The number of good days for Italian soccer in recent years is perilously small, one of the sport's most accomplished nations in the midst of a remarkable downward spiral. The two may be intertwined but even outside of Italy's inability to qualify for World Cups, Italian clubs are beginning to struggle at the Champions League level. None of them won on Matchday 2 despite facing a wide range of opponents – Roma were definitely not favored against Real Madrid but let a 3-1 lead slip away, Serie A champions Inter fell to Club Brugge while Napoli could not outdo Villarreal and Como registered a defeat at Feyenoord. Their collective record should be better on Matchday 3 but the margin by which they improve is entirely unpredictable. Inter should be favored against Shakhtar Donetsk and one might pick Roma over Slovan Bratislava. Como might have a nice opportunity in front of them against a shaky Manchester United side but there are no guarantees there, while Napoli will take on a Bodo/Glimt team that's well-positioned to give many teams a hard time.
